Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm seeking help today because I've been experiencing really severe mood swings. For about a year and two months, I felt extremely high-energy and elevated, but for the past nine months, I've fallen into a really deep depression. I just don't know how to handle these extreme shifts anymore.\n\nDoctor: During those periods when your energy was extremely high, did you ever experience things that others couldn't see or hear, or hold beliefs that were clearly not based in reality?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. Even when my energy was at its highest, I didn't see or hear things that others couldn't, nor did I hold any unrealistic beliefs.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did your mood become so intense that you required hospitalization, or were you unable to function at work or in your social life for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I was never hospitalized during that period. I'm not really sure if my daily functioning was completely disrupted to that extent, though I was definitely extremely active and constantly on the move.\n\nDoctor: While you were in that high-energy state, did you notice a significant decrease in your need for sleep, or did you find yourself talking much faster than usual and jumping quickly from one topic to another?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall having a decreased need for sleep, nor do I remember talking too fast or jumping between topics. I just felt an incredible overflow of energy and had a constant need to keep moving all the time.\n\nDoctor: Regarding that period of high energy that lasted over a year, were there any specific activities you engaged in that were risky or impulsive, such as spending excessive amounts of money or engaging in uncharacteristic behaviors?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall engaging in any risky behaviors or reckless spending during that time. While I had overflowing energy and started a lot of different activities and projects all at once, I didn't do anything dangerous or financially reckless.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months of feeling depressed, have you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, or have you had periods where you felt okay?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure about the exact daily pattern or if I had periods of feeling completely okay. However, the lack of motivation and energy has been pretty constant throughout these past nine months.\n\nDoctor: Before these past nine months, have you experienced similar periods of low mood, sadness, or lack of motivation that lasted for two years or longer without a break of more than two months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced a period of low mood or lack of motivation lasting that long before this. Prior to my struggles over the last nine months, my main issue was actually that long period of high energy.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy year, did your friends or family notice a clear change in your behavior, such as you being noticeably more talkative, energetic, or active than is typical for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, my family and friends definitely noticed a major change in me during that period. They often pointed out that I couldn't seem to stay still and appeared to have endless energy. They also mentioned how I was constantly starting multiple new activities and projects all at once.\n\nDoctor: Did this persistent increase in energy and activity significantly improve your productivity at work or in your personal projects, or did it feel more like an uncontrollable, restless state that was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: It definitely felt more like an uncontrollable, restless state rather than actual productivity. I had an intense surge of energy and started many activities at once, but I found it completely impossible to tolerate resting or staying still. It was a constant, driving need to keep moving that felt very different from my usual self."

The patient describes a prolonged period of elevated energy and activity that does not clearly meet the criteria for a full manic episode (no hospitalization, no clear impairment of functioning, no decreased need for sleep, no rapid speech/flight of ideas), which points toward hypomania, consistent with Bipolar II. Prior to my struggles over the last nine months, my main issue was actually that long period of high energy.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy year, did your friends or family notice a clear change in your behavior, such as you being noticeably more talkative, energetic, or active than is typical for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, my family and friends definitely noticed a major change in me during that period. They often pointed out that I couldn't seem to stay still and appeared to have endless energy. They also mentioned how I was constantly starting multiple new activities and projects all at once.\n\nDoctor: Did this persistent increase in energy and activity significantly improve your productivity at work or in your personal projects, or did it feel more like an uncontrollable, restless state that was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: It definitely felt more like an uncontrollable, restless state rather than actual productivity. I had an intense surge of energy and started many activities at once, but I found it completely impossible to tolerate resting or staying still. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).
